Rent-to-Own Worst Case Scenarios (and
solutions)
In t h e last i s s u e o f C a n a d i a n
Real Estate Profits I shared
with y o u o n e of the "worst
case scenarios" that can happen with your R 2 0 deals...If
o u r R 2 0 client c a n n o t or will not c o m p l e t e t h e
deal. I also shared a couple o fsolutions for that
situation.
T h e r e are five m a i n categories o f 'worst case
scenarios'...
1. O u r R 2 0 client c a n n o t or will not c o m p l e t e t h e
deal. ( S o l v e d :-)
2. T h e p r o p e r t y d o e s n o t i n c r e a s e i n v a l u e a s
anticipated.
3. T h e b a n k ( o r C M H C ) w o n ' t r e c o g n i z e t h e
rental credits/ option fee.
4. T h e b a n k (or C M H C ) c h a n g e s t h e rules part
w a y through the deal.
5.

A major issue c o m e s up with the property.

For this m o n t h ' s article, w e ' r e going t o dive into
the second scenario....
I. What happens if the property does not increase in value a s planned?
O n e of t h e m a j o r profit c e n t e r s a n d k e y s t o s u c cess in R 2 0 is being able t osell t h e h o m e t o o u r
tenant buyer a ta future price that is higher than
t h e price o f t h e h o m e w h e n t h e y first start renting
it.

T h i s i s a c o n c e r n t h a t m a n y p e o p l e h a v e in t o day's uncertain real estate m a r k e t (especially right
n o w in Alberta).
If y o u f i n d y o u r s e l f i n t h e s i t u a t i o n w h e r e y o u r t e n ant buyer h a s kept up their e n d of the bargain
(paid their rent on time, accumulated option and
rental credits, a n d i m p r o v e d their credit to the
point w h e r e they can qualify t o buy the h o u s e ) a n d t h e b a n k s a y s t h e h o u s e isn't w o r t h a s m u c h
as you and the tenant buyer agreed upon, there
are several things you can do:
1.

Extend the deal until prices reach where
you need them to. I n t h i s c a s e y o u e x p l a i n
to y o u r t e n a n t b u y e r that just b e c a u s e t h e
b a n k doesn't a g r e e o n y o u r price, a deal is a
d e a l . Y o u will a l l o w t h e m t o c o n t i n u e t o rent
t h e h o m e until t h e m a r k e t 'catches up' t o w h a t
you agreed upon. Chances are they won't b e
v e r y h a p p y a b o u t d o i n g t h a t , b u t it i s a s o l u tion.

Note: I w o u l d s u g g e s t t h a t y o u t a k e o f f t h e ' r e n t a l
credit' portion of their rent i n order to m a k e t h e
h o m e m o r e affordable for t h e m (plus they h a v e
a l r e a d y a c c u m u l a t e d all t h e y n e e d t o i n o r d e r t o
qualify t o buy t h e h o m e at t h e price y o u originally
agreed upon - so they don't need t o accumulate
any more).
2. Tenant leaves - and you either sell, rent or
rent-to-own it again. T h i s m a y h a p p e n i f t h e t e n ant buyer decides that they don't w a n t to 'wait and
see' a n d instead they c h o o s e t o m o v e o n a n d forfeit their option f e e a n d rental credits.

R E A L E S T A T E M Y W A Y - WITH K E N BEATON

Creative Big Deals
W h e n d o i n g d e a l s , big o r s m a l l , m a n y p e o p l e g e t s t u c k
in t h e b e l i e f t h a t y o u h a v e t o d o it t h e c o n v e n t i o n a l w a y
or they m a y b e using the services o f a real estate
a g e n t , lender o r lawyer w h o only think conventionally.
S o m e of the most creative deals I have structured have
been with BIG deals w h e r e I had the opportunity to
w o r k directly w i t h t h e Seller.
T o create a successful deal creatively I believe that you
n e e d t o build a relationship w i t h t h e Seller. T h i s is s o
that y o u can get to k n o w w h a t t h e R E A L motivation is
for selling the property and understand w h a t their
R E A L needs are versus what they simply want. This
can rarely b e achieved w h e n a real estate agent is involved because m o s t agents strive to prevent relationships f r o m being created for fear o f losing control of t h e
deal or for f e a r o f being e x c l u d e d f r o m t h e d e a l etc... If
an agent is involved then they should not be concerned
of being excluded f r o m the deal b e c a u s e they will h a v e
a written contract with the Seller, h o w e v e r they m a y
have a legitimate concern o f losing control and o r not
being able to protect the interest of the Seller. W i t h this
in m i n d if t h e r e i s a n a g e n t i n v o l v e d t h e n I r e c o m m e n d
y o u w o r k with t h e a g e n t a n d don't try excluding t h e
agent.
W h e n y o u a s k a n agent " w h y a r e they selling?" y o u will
often receive a very vague answer from the agent and
or the Seller. For w h a t e v e r reason m o s t people are not
willing t o s h a r e the real reason for selling b e c a u s e they
feel like they m a y b e putting t h e m s e l v e s into a poor
p o s i t i o n f o r n e g o t i a t i n g . S o in o r d e r t o h a v e t h e S e l l e r
o p e n u p a n d in o r d e r f o r y o u t o l e a r n w h a t t h e R E A L
m o t i v a t i o n is w i l l r e q u i r e y o u t o d e v e l o p a r e l a t i o n s h i p .
N o w I a m not suggesting y o u b e c o m e best friends but
you should meet several times, perhaps once at the
property and then take t h e m for a coffee another time
etc... a n d a s k questions about the Seller and his/her
f a m i l y a s w e l l a s t h e p r o p e r t y . It i s i m p o r t a n t t o n o t
m a k e it a l l b u s i n e s s b y m a k i n g s o m e s m a l l t a l k i n o r d e r
to start building a relationship. W h e n talking about the
property m a k e c o m p l i m e n t a r y c o m m e n t s to help build
their pride and m a k e t h e m feel g o o d about their situation. Build trust a n d e v e n t u a l l y t h e S e l l e r will a s k a b o u t
y o u w h i c h will t h e n give y o u t h e opportunity to explain
your story. Y o u r ultimate goal is to create a relationship
w h e r e the Seller w a n t s to help y o u achieve y o u r goal
while at the s a m e time they achieve their R E A L goal(s)
m a k i n g it a w i n / w i n f o r e v e r y o n e .
Legal v s Illegal!!! V T B ( V e n d e r T a k e B a c k ) is illegal,
C a s h B a c k is illegal, a n d h a v i n g t h e S e l l e r pay m y

c o s t s a r e illegal, etc T h e
t e r m "illegal" is u s e d far t o o
m u c h and often incorrectly
because people don't k n o w
t h e r u l e s o r if a p e r s o n i s n o t
familiar with a t e r m their autom a t i c r e s p o n s e i s " y o u c a n ' t d o t h a t o r it i s i l l e g a l . . . " .
W h e n a person is presented with an opportunity that
is c o n f u s i n g o r if t h e y a r e n o t k n o w l e d g e a b l e a b o u t
t h e d e t a i l s t h e n it i s a n a t u r a l r e s p o n s e t o s i m p l y r e spond with " N O " and reject the opportunity. R a t h e r
than taking the time to learn about these special conditions m o s t S e l l e r s a n d real e s t a t e a g e n t s will s a y
"that can't b e d o n e or t h a t it is illegal o r w e s i m p l y
d o n ' t w a n t t o d o i t . . . " M o s t a g e n t s h a v e v e r y little
experience with creative d e a l s a n d m a n y will r e c o m m e n d to the Seller not to entertain any creative deal
because they simply don't understand the deals. T h i s
is u n d e r s t a n d a b l e b e c a u s e a s r e a l e s t a t e a g e n t s w e
are not taught about creative strategies and w e are
e n c o u r a g e d a s r e a l t o r s t o a v o i d t h e m . T h i s is v e r y
important for you a s an investor to understand bec a u s e y o u will often n e e d t o w o r k w i t h t h e realtor a n d
you should know and understand what your chall e n g e w i l l b e s o t h a t y o u c a n a p p r o a c h t h e d e a l differently.
S o h o w do you o v e r c o m e these potential road blocks
with real estate agents a n d Sellers? T h e simple a n s w e r is " e d u c a t i o n " . Y o u n e e d t o k n o w a n d u n d e r stand w h a t the others o n the other side of the deal
k n o w and understand regarding the creative deal.
Y o u may need toeducate the agent and you may
need to educate the Seller which can b e a challenge
if t h e y d o n ' t w a n t t o b e e d u c a t e d . W h e n y o u a r e u n able to build a relationship w i t h t h e Seller t h e n w h a t I
suggest you d o is provide a written d o c u m e n t

ORIS AT BUSINESS, GREAT LINE WITH ANDREW BARBER-STARKEY

"The Next Big Thing
Recently Iw a s talking t o m y wife
about the world ofcoaching and
personal development. S h e said t o
me, "I g u e s s t h e d a y s o f getting
1000 people t o attend a free seminar on h o w to m a k e a million dollars are over." S h e ' s
right. I n m y e x p e r i e n c e m o s t o f t h e 'get rich quick'
crowd in North America are either well on their w a y t o
achieving their goals o r h a s s o m u c h debt f r o m trying
to g e t rich t h a t t h e y a r e f i n a n c i a l l y a n d e m o t i o n a l l y
broke.

W h e n y o u look into a mirror y o u s e e a direct reflection o fyour o w n image coming back atyou. Y o u r
mind works the s a m e way. T h e thoughts you have
are invisible but t h e y actually create the world that
c o m e s back a t y o u . T h e tricky part is that there is a
delayed reaction inthe 'mirror' o fyour thoughts.
Y o u r mind y o u can change your experience o f reality i n s t a n t l y b e c a u s e it i s v i r t u a l a n d i n t a n g i b l e .
Transforming your external world takes longer because physical matter is denser and slower to
change than thought.

T h e n s h e a s k e d , "I w o n d e r w h a t T h e N e x t Big T h i n g '
will b e ? " T h e a n s w e r i m m e d i a t e l y leaped into m y
h e a d a n d I s a i d , " I k n o w w h a t i t i s a n d it i s g o i n g t o
h a v e a g r e a t e r i m p a c t t h a n t h e internet, cell p h o n e s
and the personal computer."

Every negative thought takes you further from the
world y o u m o s t deeply desire. A n d every positive
thought brings y o u closer. T h i s is w h y m a n a g i n g
your o w n mind is paramount! Y o u r goal should b e
to s y s t e m a t i c a l l y t r a n s f o r m y o u r n e g a t i v e t h o u g h t s
into positive o n e s . E a c h t i m e y o u d o y o u r world will
improve - although you m u s t b e patient because
the i m p r o v e m e n t is subject to the delay inthe mirror.

Think for a m o m e n t . W h a t could have T H A T m u c h
i m p a c t ? Y e s , T h e N e x t Big T h i n g ' is t h e ability t o
unleash the potential o f our o w n minds. I a m sure y o u
a r e a w a r e that y o u h a v e t h e ability t o a c h i e v e far
m o r e than y o u do. A n d you k n o w that the biggest obstacle holding y o u back and preventing y o u from
reaching m o r e o fyour potential isyour o w n mind. But
k n o w i n g t h i s j u s t a d d s t o y o u r f r u s t r a t i o n if y o u d o n ' t
know how release your o w n blockages!
C l e a r l y o n e n e w s l e t t e r w o n ' t t e a c h y o u all y o u n e e d t o
k n o w in order to master the domain o f mind power
a n d q u a n t u m p h y s i c s - but it's a start. I h a v e b e e n
deeply i m m e r s e d in this world for the past t w o years.
Although I have barely scratched the surface, I a m
already experiencing results with m y coaching clients
a n d i n m y o w n life t h a t a r e n o t h i n g s h o r t o f m i r a c u lous. After 2 3 y e a r s o fcoaching clients t o achieve
their goals through strategic plans and actions, I n o w
realize that mindset a n d thought patterns are far m o r e
important than strategy.
T h i s i s b e c a u s e t h e world w e live in is like a mirror.

The w a y t o h a r n e s s this power is t o b e c o m e a w a r e
of your negative thoughts and emotions. Here is a
partial list o f t h o u g h t s y o u w a n t t o n o t i c e a n d t r a n s f o r m : guilt, fear, a n g e r , criticism of y o u r s e l f o r o t h ers, a n n o y a n c e , a n x i e t y a n d stress, feelings o f
d o u b t , b l a m e , a n y a n d all c o m p l a i n t s , s c a r c i t y a n d
lack, obligation a n d r e s e n t m e n t .
T o b e clear, I a m not talking about 'positive thinking'
in w h i c h y o u c o v e r o v e r n e g a t i v e t h i n k i n g a n d e m o t i o n s w i t h a layer o f h a p p y t h o u g h t s . T h a t is like
putting a n e w coat o f paint o v e r rotting w o o d a n d is
the primary reason that the L a w of Attraction and
o t h e r m a n i f e s t a t i o n t e c h n i q u e s fail for m o s t people.
T h e key ist o genuinely experience positive
thoughts and e m o t i o n s including abundance, acceptance and especially gratitude.
I'll g e t i n t o t h i s m o r e d e e p l y i n a f u t u r e n e w s l e t t e r
but for n o w , h e r e is a helpful assignment. Monitor
your mind and d oyour best to b ea w a r e of your
negative thinking. W h e n you notice any negative
t h o u g h t , a v o i d b e a t i n g y o u r s e l f u p f o r it. T h a t i s s e l f criticism and m a k e s things w o r s e . S i m p l y accept
that you are being negative and celebrate your
a w a r e n e s s . T h a t celebration is t h e first s t e p to t u r n ing things around.

B U Y I T AND KEEP IT

BY JULY ONO

Enter With A n Exit
W h a t a r e y o u doing sitting o n t h e side lines? G e t in
t h e g a m e . I'll t e l l y o u w h y . I w a s c o n t a c t e d b y s o m e one that I had met over ten years ago a t an event.
W e w e r e both in t h e s a m e financial position. T h i s
s o m e o n e has been watching m y progress over the
years. T h i s s o m e o n e h a s been in the s a m e place for
the past decade. Today, the financial difference bet w e e n u s i s a c h a s m . It a l l s t a r t s w i t h s m a l l a c t i o n
steps, t a k e n daily, that a c c u m u l a t e e v e r y w e e k , that
turn into m o n t h s , that turn into years, t o get t h e res u l t s . It's n e v e r t o o l a t e t o g e t i n t h e g a m e . S t o p
watching others. Y o u n e e d t o start taking informed
r i s k s , f e e l t h e f e a r a n d d o it a n y w a y .
In a n o t h e r e x a m p l e , I w a s m e n t o r i n g a y o u n g l a d y t o
get into h e r first property but s h e h a s t h e classic e x cuse o f n o m o n e y . H e r boyfriend is a perfectionist
h a n d y m a n . H e builds beautiful finishes but then n o
o n e i s a l l o w e d t o t o u c h it o r u s e it. N o t v e r y p r a c t i c a l
f o r a r e n t a l a s it w i l l g e t u s e d a n d a b u s e d b y t e n a n t s .
H e is a n a n c h o r for her financial plans. W h e n I asked
a b o u t m a k i n g s u r e their first p u r c h a s e h a s a m o r t g a g e helper, her r e s p o n s e t o m e w a s that option w a s
off the table. H e gets creeped out by e v e n thinking
a b o u t h a v i n g a s t r a n g e r living d o w n s t a i r s . W e l l it's
obvious that h e has n o plan for financial freedom. H e
is n o t w i l l i n g t o m a k e s a c r i f i c e s i n t h e s h o r t t e r m f o r
the long t e r m gain. S h e has t o face the cold hard
truth that his goals are not aligned with her goals. H e
is a n e m o t i o n a l i n v e s t o r a n d m a k e s d e c i s i o n s o n h o w
h e feels. S h e is a n u m b e r c r u n c h e r a n d m a k e s decis i o n s b a s e d o n n u m b e r s . S h e h a s t h e ability t o p u t
her e m o t i o n s t o the side. T h i s is a great characteristic
for a real estate investor t o have.
I shared the story o f h o w e v e n after I retired f r o m
having t o work, with millions o f dollars o f real estate
under m a n a g e m e n t , S t e v e and I chose t o rent a fourplex. W e had great n e i g h b o u r s . All o f o u r c a s h f l o w
w a s b e i n g d i v e r t e d t o a c q u i r i n g p a s s i v e i n c o m e . If w e
had b o u g h t a primary residence, t h e m o r t g a g e interest portion w o u l d not b e tax deductible. T h e mortgage interest is the largest e x p e n s e . W e couldn't justify h a v i n g a h o u s e w i t h s u c h a l o n g p a y b a c k h o r i z o n .
W e w a n t e d t o l e v e r a g e o u r m o n e y in o r d e r t o accelerate and multiply our financial goals. W e waited for
t h e right t i m i n g , t h e right m a r k e t , s a v e d u p m o n e y t o
pay for a h o u s e without a mortgage, before w e
m o v e d out o f the four-plex.
After w e closed o n the house, w e turned around a n d
took out the m a x i m u m Line O f Credit ( L O C ) that w a s
a v a i l a b l e t w o d a y s later. T h a t L O C w a s t h e n u s e d t o

invest in another apartm e n t creating m o r e cash
flow.
This is not a decision
that most people would
m a k e . It w a s o u r j o i n t d e c i s i o n . W e c o u l d h a v e e a s e d
into a residence by purchasing a h o u s e with a b a s e m e n t
suite, o r buy a duplex and live in o n e side w h i l e w e
rented o u t t h e o t h e r side, o r b o u g h t a four-plex a n d live
in o n e u n i t a n d r e n t o u t t h e o t h e r t h r e e . W e c o u l d h a v e
created a private equity pool with family and friends t o
c o m e up with the d o w n payment, then jointly buy a
h o u s e , l i v e i n it f o r a f e w y e a r s , t h e n flip f o r p r o f i t a n d
split t h e c a p i t a l g a i n . T h e s e w e r e o p t i o n s t h a t w e c o n templated. Again, only the pro-rated interest portion o n
t h e rental is tax deductible. W e w a n t e d all o f t h e m o r t gage interest t o b e tax deductible.
Y e s , y o u d o n e e d a n exit strategy b e f o r e y o u buy, b u t if
y o u d o n ' t t a k e a c t i o n , it's k i n d o f p o i n t l e s s . W h a t i s a n
exit s t r a t e g y ? A s I w a s e x p l a i n i n g t o t h e y o u n g lady, I
a s k e d her w h o is going t o buy the property w h e n s h e
p u t s it u p f o r s a l e . I s it a h o m e o w n e r o r i n v e s t o r ? I s it
going t o b e a family with young children? W i t h older children? A single with a pet? A single without pets? W h a t
demographic are you attracting? W h i t e collar? Blue collar? W h a t kind o f car d o e s your ideal buyer drive? L o o k
in t h e d r i v e w a y o f t h e s t r e e t w h e r e y o u a r e p l a n n i n g t o
buy. W h a t type o f vehicles are parked in the d r i v e w a y ?
On the street? Trucks or Corollas or B M W ' s ? Once you
d e t e r m i n e w h o will buy y o u r property, t h e n y o u w o r k
backwards from the Exit Point to your Entry Point. N o w
y o u h a v e t h e a c t i o n s t e p s laid o u t b e f o r e y o u t o w a r d s
your end result.
T h i s is w h a t could happen without a well-defined exit
strategy. T h e r e is a b e d & b r e a k f a s t ( B & B ) for s a l e b u t
the land is leased. T h e o w n e r is selling the building a n d
the business.

circumstances

A t it, I h a d t h e p l e a s u r e o f h e a r i n g f r o m n o t o n e ,
but t w o different R 2 0 students w h o are successfully running their R 2 0 b u s i n e s s e s f r o m a distance.

C r a i g h a s it a l l s e t u p s o t h a t h e n e v e r a c t u a l l y
m e e t s o r speaks with the tenant buyer!

Craig Lindgaard, f r o m R i c h m o n d B C , h a s h i s
R 2 0 b u s i n e s s g o i n g s t r o n g w a a a a y o v e r in O n tario. In fact, y o u c o u l d a l m o s t call h i s s y s t e m ,
"The Lazy Man's Way to R20 Riches" ( N o i n s u l t
intended...! admire h o w he h a s set things up t o
truly a u t o m a t e a s m u c h as h e possibly can!).
H e a d v e r t i s e s f o r p r o s p e c t i v e R 2 0 c l i e n t s i n Kijiji i n
his Ontario market, and then only deals with the
ones w h o look the most promising. He emails
t h e m t oconfirm s o m e of the information they sent
h i m in their initial application, a n d o n l y f o l l o w s u p
with the o n e s w h o respond back to him (and surprisingly, quite a f e w do not... s o h e doesn't w a s t e
any time chasing those ones down).

mortgage broker (team m e m b e r ) in the area w h o
further pre-screens t h e m t os e e w h a t their current
credit level is, w h a t t h e y n e e d t o d o i n o r d e r t o get

Raul Rivera f r o m V i c t o r i a a l s o r u n s a l o n g - d i s t a n c e
R 2 0 business, focusing on doing deals in E d m o n t o n
(where he focuses on the Latino market).
H e d o e s things differently t h a n Craig, but with t h e
s a m e end results. Raul pays a monthly fee to have
a 'virtual' office in E d m o n t o n . After c o m m u n i c a t i n g
w i t h t h e p r o s p e c t i v e t e n a n t - b u y e r s by e m a i l at t h e
b e g i n n i n g , w h e n it is t i m e to g e t d o w n t o b u s i n e s s ,
h e h a s t h e m g o to his 'office' w h e r e a staff m e m b e r
helps t h e m get logged o n to a computer for a face-to

local t e a m m e m b e r s to get things u p a n d running but this w a y h e gets a g o o d 'feel" for the prospects
and has actually s e e n t h e m and spoken with t h e m .

B o r n T o B e W i l d „.?

September was one fun and crazy month
baby! I celebrated birthday number 4-7
in Kamloops with my kids (wow! I'm
getting up there!). The next day I flew
off to San Francisco to meet up with my
girlfriend Kiri (do we call people 'girlfriends' when we are middle aged? Is
'Ladyfriend' more age-appropriate? I
dunno). Kiri is from New Zealand and
she was wrapping up her year-long visit
to North America and we decided to meet
up in San Fran before she left for down
under.
I had only driven through San Francisco
once, and that was 25 years ago, so it
was all new to me. What an amazing
city!

not be as scenic, but it is a much longer,
more impressive bridge (8 miles total) as
well as being the busiest bridge in North
America (500K cars a day cross it!).
We had a personal walking tour of China
Town, and we went and watched a U.S.
college football game (with over 30,000
people in the stands!) between Berkley
and a Louisiana team. Even though neither one of us understands American
football, it was a blast! My favorite part
was watching the Louisiana team's marching band. There were over 200 people
in it, all in uniform, all in tune and all
very coordinated with their jazzy choreography as well.

Toured the infamous Alcatraz and that
We stayed at a great little hotel right
was very interesting and eye opening
down in the financial district (close to
(glad I wasn't 'visiting' that place 50
Chinatown, little Italy, and not far from
years ago as an inmate...not fun). There
the waterfront either). I highly recomwas actually one of the old inmates
mend doing the 'touristy' thing and getthere, signing copies of his book about
ting a pass for the 'hop on, hop off bus his experiences there. Not too many of

and all the main touristy places in
I think our favorite part of the city is
S.F.. I was actually more impressed with North Beach (little Italy). Lot's of great
the Oakland Bay bridge myself. It might
cafe's, bars and restaurants. We stopped
San Francisco Trip - Sept. 2015

III

Page 1

in at a little hole-in-the-wall cafe that is
famous for being the place where Francis Ford Coppola wrote the screenplay
for "The Godfather" (he has his own
building, film studio and restaurant just
down the street now).
I've travelled a lot, and this was one of
the best trips of my life, and five days
was just the right amount of time. We
got to see pretty much everything we
wanted to, and we were left wanting to
go back and explore more.
It was sad to bid farewell to lovely
Kiri, and I'm planning to go visit her
in New Zealand next Spring.
I didn't have very long to feel sorry
for myself because a week after returning from San Fran, I was on a plane to
Edmonton to meet Ken Beaton and start
our "5 Homes To Freedom Alberta
Tour" (photos below and on last page).
We did a whirlwind jaunt through the
province, putting on events in Medicine
Hat, Lethbridge, Calgary, Red Deer and
Edmonton.
It's always fun to spend time with
Ken.
We give each other a hard time,
but the truth of the matter is that we
get along and work together very well.
We had a little break over the weekend
and stayed with our friends Mark and
Lyla Phillips in Fort Saskatchewan (just
outside of Edmonton). Mark and Lyla
are serious entrepreneurs and own a
couple of businesses as well as some
residential real estate.
One of their enterprises is
running very successful concessions at trade shows, fairs,
and at the Commonwealth Stadium when the Edmonton Eskimo's are playing. Mark got
us some tickets to go see the
game between the Eskimo's
and the BC Lions. I'm not a
football fan, but it was a lot
of fun to watch the game and see how
everything works. I was also very im-

pressed with Mark and Lyla's concession

We wrapped up our tour with a 'bang' in

Then I'm holding different Massive Implementation Bootcamps (M.I.B.'s) in Kamloops mid-month. I'll be holding my last
R20 M.I.B. Oct. 20-21, and then my first
Raising Capital M.I.B. right after that,

And to wrap up the month, Ken and I
will be putting on the "5 Homes to Freedom 3 Day Bootcamp" in Edmonton, Oct.
30 - Nov. 1st. This is a complimentary

Autumn is here, summer is over. School

R E A L E S T A T E M Y W A Y - WITH K E N BEATON

Creative BIG Deal #1
C a n you do a creative deal with a corporation?
W h e n purchasing an investment property m a n y
investors will p u r c h a s e t h e property within a corporation and this has both pros and cons. O w n e r s
will d e c i d e to place their property within a c o r p o r a t i o n f e e l i n g t h a t it p r o v i d e s m o r e p r o t e c t i o n f r o m
litigation (not n e c e s s a r i l y t r u e in C a n a d a ) . B u y e r s
wish to purchase within a corporation for the financial b e n e f i t s of t h e t a x a t i o n rates. P u r c h a s i n g a
property within a Corporation isa personal choice
which you should discuss with your accountant
and lawyer. S o w h y bring up corporations?
O f t e n t i m e s a Seller w i s h e s to sell their investment
property through a corporation which m e a n s the
Buyer purchases the Corporation which holds the
title of t h e property. T h e S e l l e r likely p l a c e d t h e
property within a corporate structure w h e n they
purchased the property and then operated the
property within the corporation for a n u m b e r of
y e a r s until t h e y d e c i d e d to sell t h e property. T h e
Seller h a s t w o options; Option # 1 - sell t h e property and dissolve the corporation, or Option # 2 sell the actual corporation with the property inside
it. H e r e a r e s o m e P r o s a n d C o n s f o r b o t h t h e
Seller and Buyer to consider w h e n selling/buying
a corporation:
Pros:
Buyer: A s t h e B u y e r o f a c o r p o r a t i o n t h e r e
is n o l a n d t r a n s f e r t a x t o b e p a i d b e c a u s e
t h e property title i s not c h a n g i n g h a n d s
and this can be a significant saving. O n a
$ 1 , 0 0 0 , 0 0 0 p r o p e r t y in O n t a r i o t h a t c a n
save the buyer over $16,000.
Seller: F r o m t h e S e l l e r s p e r s p e c t i v e s e l l ing a corporation is g o o d b e c a u s e there is
less potential Capital Gains to be realized.
In fact t h e r e m a y be z e r o tax payable o n
the sale because of the business tax credits. A n y p o t e n t i a l liability i s s u e s m a y a l s o
be minimized w h e n selling a corporation
because the new shareholder takes on
t h e s e liabilities.
Cons:
Buyer: T h e o l d s t o r y o f " B u y e r B e W a r e "

holds true here because when a
Buyer purchases a
corporation they are
also inheriting the
history of the corporation. T h i s m e a n s
t h a t if t h e r e i s a t a x a u d i t f r o m 5 y e a r s
ago and the 1414455 O N T A R I O INC
new O w n e r has only owned the corporation for a year then the 1414455 O N T A R I O I N C will b e held a c c o u n t a b l e for
the o u t c o m e of the audit. T h e s a m e
thing can apply with respect to environmental issues that are discovered after
t h e transfer of o w n e r s h i p of t h e property.
For these reasons y o u would w a n t to b e
sure that y o u h a v e a very detailed audit
(such as a tax audit and environmental
a s s e s s m e n t e t c . . ) of the property perf o r m e d by qualified specialists.
F r o m a tax savings position the Buyer
s h o u l d a l s o r e a l i z e t h a t if t h e y p l a n t o
depreciate the property for tax purposes
t h e n t h e v a l u e to d e p r e c i a t e will not b e
set at t h e s a m e price t h e y paid for t h e
C o r p o r a t i o n . T h e depreciation rate will
be at w h a t e v e r t h e r e m a i n i n g b a l a n c e o f
t h e p r o p e r t y v a l u e i s in t h e f i n a n c i a l
b o o k s of t h e corporation at t h e t i m e of
the sale which m a y be considerably
lower than the actual purchase price o f
the corporation.

mind. W h a t ' s great about this isthat y o u have abs o l u t e c o n t r o l i n t h i s a r e a . Y o u c a n d o it u n d e r a n y
c i r c u m s t a n c e s ; it i s i n s t a n t a n e o u s a n d i t ' s f r e e o f

c h a n g e y o u r m i n d t o a n attitude of gratitude. E v e r y
o n e of t h e m isextremely powerful and the deeper

e x p e r i e n c e o f life will a u t o m a t i c a l l y i m p r o v e . O v e r

Y o u can find plenty o finformation a n d practices
related t o this level of gratitude in y o u r local
bookstore and on-line. Keeping a gratitude journ a l o r w r i t i n g a list o f t h i n g s y o u a r e g r a t e f u l f o r
each day issurprisingly powerful. S o is expressing gratitude to o t h e r s a n d looking for t h e positive
in difficult s i t u a t i o n s . T h e s e p r a c t i c e s a l o n e will
c h a n g e y o u r life.
But there's a second, m o r e transformative level
of practicing gratitude. At this level, instead o f
thinking about what you are grateful for y o u actua l l y e x p e r i e n c e it i n y o u r b o d y . T r y t h i s e x e r c i s e
now. Bring t o mind something o rs o m e o n e y o u
a r e g r a t e f u l for. C r e a t e a m e n t a l list o f all t h e
things related t o that person or thing for which
y o u are grateful. N o w close your e y e s and g o
beyond thinking; feel the gratitude inyour body.

y o u will n o t i c e a f e e l i n g o f b e i n g lifted u p t h a t
raises your energetic state. This increases and
r e p r o g r a m s y o u for greater success a ta cellular
level.

BY JULY ONO

You A r eT h eS u mTotal Of Your Choices
Y o u r life t o d a y i s t h e r e s u l t o f t h e s u m t o t a l o f
choices that y o u h a v e m a d e over your lifetime.
T h i n k o f y o u r t i m e line a s a line d r a w n f r o m left
t o right w i t h y o u r birth a t t h e left a n d y o u r life

R E A L T Y L E G A L I T I E S WITH B A R R Y M C G U I R E
TAKING CARE OF

O u r client found six 4 - p l e x e s in a s m a l l Alberta, r e source- driven t o w n . H e looked a t s o m e o f the statist i c s a n d s a w t h a t , l i k e a lot o f s m a l l t o w n s t h a t d e p e n d
on the resource industry, rental rates and vacancy
rates w e r e e x t r e m e l y cyclical. W h e n industry w a s
booming, rents were upand vacancies were minimal. A t the bottom o f t h e cycle, vacancies w e r e high
and rents w e r e low.
H e bought the units f r o m a bank w h o had taken t h e m
back in foreclosure. F i n a n c i n g w a s provided b y the
f o r e c l o s i n g b a n k , w h o w e r e g l a d t o g e t rid o f t h e
units. T h e price per d o o r w a s excellent a n d t h e t o w n ' s
resource industry cycle w a s o n the upswing. V a c a n cies w e r e n o w relatively low and cash flow w a s posit i v e . T h e r e w e r e m o n e y p a r t n e r s t o p r o v i d e all t h e
cash required. Detailed discussions w e r e held with
the m o n e y partners t o m a k e sure they understood the
cyclical nature o f vacancies a n d rental rates. A Joint
V e n t u r e A g r e e m e n t w a s signed, our client closed o n
the property and m a n a g e m e n t c o m m e n c e d .
O v e r the next f e w years, predictable events occurred. T h e town's resource industry b o o m e d for a
while but t h e n w e n t in the other direction. V a c a n c i e s
went up and rents w e n t down.
In a r e l a t i v e l y s h o r t p e r i o d o f t i m e :
1.) T h e t h r e e - y e a r t e r m e x p i r e d o n f i n a n c i n g c a r r i e d b y
t h e f o r e c l o s i n g seller b a n k . T h e b a n k did not w a n t t o
r e n e w the mortgage. T h e y only carried the financing
originally b e c a u s e that w a s the only w a y they could
sell t h e property. T h e w h o l e c o u n t r y w a s in a bit o f a
credit crunch, similar t o w h a t w e are facing now. O u r
client found a s u b - p r i m e lender w h o provided a n e x pensive mortgage for o n e year only. W h e n that year
w a s o v e r t h e y didn't w a n t t o r e n e w either. N o o t h e r
lender w o u l d step up. O u r client explained the situation t o s o m e o f his relatives w h o had available
cash. H e w a s able t o get a private mortgage from his
family t o replace the bank mortgage. H e did not ask
his m o n e y partners for further contributions.

2.) W h e n the resource cycle w a s high, these units
threw off great positive cash flow and distributions
w e r e m a d e t o the m o n e y partners. W h e n the cycle
w a s low t h e r e w a s negative c a s h flow a n d o u r client
put u p a n y f u n d s required f r o m his o w n pocket. H e
did not a s k his m o n e y p a r t n e r s f o r e x t r a c o n t r i b u t i o n s
although the joint venture a g r e e m e n t said that h e

BUSINESS

could.
3.) E v e r y year, w i t h o u t fail a n d
without asking, our client m a d e
s u r e his m o n e y partners received a n a n n u a l financial report with our client's current
analysis o f the investment. H e also provided the C a n a d a R e v e n u e A g e n c y s t a t e m e n t o f real estate rentals
that the m o n e y partners w o u l d need t o get t o their accountants t o report t h e investment o n their tax returns.
I a s k e d o u r client w h y h e didn't a s k for help w h e n :
A. cash injection f r o m m o n e y partners would h a v e
helped out o n the financing side, a n d
B. c a s h f l o w w a s n e g a t i v e h e p u t i n a n y f u n d s r e q u i r e d .
H e s a i d it w a s h i s o w n p h i l o s o p h i c a l v i e w o f J o i n t V e n tures. E v e n though h e could have asked for m o n e y according t o the Joint V e n t u r e Agreement, h e took the position that his m o n e y partners w e r e expecting him t o
provide a trouble-free Joint Venture. T h a t w a s the reputation that h e h a d built u p a n d that h a d a l l o w e d h i m t o
acquire a large portfolio. S o m e i n v e s t m e n t s w o r k e d better t h a n o t h e r s a n d in m o s t c a s e s h e didn't require extra
c o n t r i b u t i o n s . T h i s o n e w a s a little d i f f e r e n t , b u t h i s
reputation a n d portfolio- building ability d e p e n d e d o n
'trouble-free'.
P L E A S E N O T E , this is a particular point o f v i e w that o u r
client i m p l e m e n t e d . W e ' r e not suggesting that this is t h e
w a y y o u s h o u l d run y o u r Joint V e n t u r e s . T a k e this inform a t i o n a s the w a y o n e client runs his.
LESSONS LEARNED:
1. B e ready for trouble. J V r e q u i r e m e n t s aside, h o w will
you react?
2. Y o u r J V A m u s t deal with negative issues a s well a s
positive.
3. U n d e r s t a n d t h e i m p o r t a n c e o f r e p u t a t i o n t o J V s u c cess.
#END#

A D E A agent pulled
into a Texas ranch
and knocked o n the
door. "T need to i n spect y o u r land f o r
illegally g r o w n drugs," h etold the
rancher.
"Well, y o u can look wherever y o u
want," the rancher replied. " B u t y o u
have t ostay out o f the north field."
T h e agent reached into his pocket and
pulled out his badge. "Listen, this
badge gives m e authorityto g o wherever I w a n t o n y o u r land, s odon't tell
m e where 1 can and can't go! Understand?"
"Certainly." T h e rancher backed away.
H e watched as the agent searched his
property, until finally they reached the
north field. T h e agent c l i m b e d over a
fence and started to w a l k .
Suddenly a huge bull spotted h i m and
began t o charge. T h e agent ran, but the
bull was catching up.
"Help!" h e shouted to the rancher.
"What d oI do?"
T h e rancher leaned
over the fence.
"Show h i m your
badge! S h o w h i m
your badge!"

that they were both happy with, and i t
truly is light years a w a y f r o m w h a t I
started with.
I did m y t a l k a t Ted's event, and r e a l l y
enjoyed doing it. I t w o r k e d well, and I got
great feedback f r o m the group ( a n d f r o m
Ted... w h i c h i s a big source o f pride f o r m e
personally).
And I took away FIVE important
f r o m the whole experience.

lessons

1 . I ' m n o t as g o o d as I t h o u g h t I w a s .
I have t a k e n quite a bit o f training, a n d
invested a lot o f money and time learning
h o w t o do presentations.
I have gotten
pretty
good r e sults
from
them i n
the past.
But at
the e n d
of the
day, having the
chance
to w o r k
with
someone
who is a
"Master"
really
showed
m e h o w m u c h f u r t h e r I h a v e t o go t o become t r u l y "good".
Ted says that i t w a s as i f I w e n t f r o m
'high school' right into a Master's program!
2. B e open t o feedback ( b o t h good A N D
bad). I had t o fight h a r d against m y
bruised little ego f r o m t i m e t o t i m e during
the w h o l e process. I t w o u l d h a v e been
easy t o s a y 'screw it' and stick t o w h a t I
w a s comfortable doing instead o f accepting
t h e i r feedback w i t h a n open m i n d .
3. A l w a y s h a v e a 'learners attitude'.
When y o u think y o u k n o w i t all, y o u are
screwed! Ted h a s h a da w i n n i n g presentation that hasworked extremely well for 3 0
years. H e still tweaks i t and changes i t a
little bit e v e r y t i m e h e does i t . H e t o l d m e
that h e actually used a f e w things h e s a w
i n one o f m y presentations and started using t h e m i n his! Ted is a master a t this,

Page 2

©

and h e continues t o learn and improve.
4. Little hinges m o v e big doors (but t h e y
can be h a r d t o screw in!). I frustrated m y self m o r e t h a n a n y t h i n g b y not i n t e r n a l i z i n g
some v e r y basic concepts t h a t Ted w a s teaching me... and h a v i n g t o learn the same thing
repeatedly u n t i l i t finally 'stuck' i n m y t h i c k
skull! Only t o find that i t w o u l d 'unstick'
and I ' d have t o be reminded about i t again.
Ted showed m e some v e r y i m p o r t a n t distinctions that I a m still w o r k i n g hard t o internalize. I realized t h a t seeing, hearing, a n d e v e n
doing s o m e t h i n g j u s t once ( o r e v e n 2 o r 3
times) is not usually enough t o have i t really
stick. I t takes a lot m o r e t h a n t h a t f o r i t t o
really become part o f m y subconscious.
5. T h e v a l u e o f
having a coach/
trainer/mentor. I
h a v e a l w a y s appreciated h a v i n g a
coach and a m e n tor. I've invested
many, many, many
thousands o f dollars i n professionals i n t h e past.
But it's been
a w h i l e since I ' v e
actually w o r k e d
w i t h one personally. This experience brought back
the importance o f
personal coaching t o m e .

Key #1: Disclosure
T h e first thing investors n e e d t o realize is that their
mortgage professional should b e playing o n their t e a m .
A n independent mortgage agent or broker has access
to s e v e r a l different l e n d e r s , a n d will b e a b l e t o help y o u
t a k e y o u r d e a l s t o t h e right l e n d e r s t o best realize y o u r

f r o m painting yourself into a proverbial corner with respect to your financing.
The mistake m a n y investors m a k e with regard t o disclosure is that t h e y withhold information b e c a u s e t h e y
think sharing certain details m a y jeopardize their deal.
Instead, the opposite should be the case. S m a r t invest o r s d i s c l o s e all o f t h e i r d e t a i l s t o t h e i r m o r t g a g e p r o f e s s i o n a l a n d let t h e m d o t h e i r j o b i n t h e i r a r e a o f e x pertise.

rents, etc.?

m o r e important. Y o u r approvals from lenders are only

situation, h o w can you expect your mortgage professional, a c c o u n t a n t o r l a w y e r t o give y o u t h e right a d vice?

What is a Property Really
I a m currently w o r k i n g with a client w h o is looking at a
large commercial property t o purchase and h e has discovered that the B a n k has a very different opinion o f the
v a l u e c o m p a r e d t o w h a t t h e S e l l e r b e l i e v e s it t o b e w o r t h .
S o h o w m u c h is it t r u l y w o r t h ? T h i s i s a q u e s t i o n I o f t e n
r e c e i v e a n d i s s o m e t i m e s ( o r o f t e n ) difficult t o a n s w e r .
T h e b o t t o m line is that t h e v a l u e is ultimately d e t e r m i n e d
w h e n a willing buyer a n d a willing seller enter into a m u tually agreed contract with neither party experiencing a n y
u n d u e stress o r p r e s s u r e t o sell o r buy. T h e challenge
lies in getting the lenders t o a g r e e with this!
O k a y , y o u h a v e a willing Seller a n d Buyer looking objectively a t a property with n o outside forces playing a factor! S o h o w d o t h e y d e t e r m i n e t h e v a l u e ? W i t h a s i n g l e
family h o m e t h e v a l u e is m o s t often d e t e r m i n e d b a s e d
upon comparable sales, yet there are t i m e s w h e n a willing b u y e r m a y p a y m o r e f o r a p r o p e r t y t h a n w h a t t h e
m a r k e t s u g g e s t s it i s w o r t h if t h e b u y e r h a s a s p e c i f i c
n e e d o r p u r p o s e f o r t h e p r o p e r t y o r if t h e y b e c o m e e m o tionally involved with the property.
W h e n determining the value o f a commercial property,
such a s a n a p a r t m e n t building, t h e v a l u e is determined
b a s e d u p o n its a b i l i t y t o c r e a t e a profit. A g a i n t h e r e m a y
still b e t h e o c c a s i o n a l t i m e w h e n a b u y e r i s w i l l i n g t o p a y
m o r e f o r a c o m m e r c i a l p r o p e r t y if t h e y h a v e a s p e c i f i c
n e e d for the property, but for the m o s t part the v a l u e is
based upon the financial performance of the property
a n d i t s ability t o g e n e r a t e a p o s i t i v e c a s h f l o w .
S o l e t ' s r e v i e w h o w v a l u e is d e t e r m i n e d b y c a s h f l o w a n d
profit. T o d e t e r m i n e t h e c a s h f l o w f o r a p r o p e r t y s i m p l y
t a k e all o f t h e r e v e n u e s c o l l e c t e d a n d d e d u c t all o f t h e
related e x p e n s e s such a s property tax, property insura n c e , utilities, e t c . . . a n d t h i s i s c a l l e d t h e " N e t O p e r a t i n g
I n c o m e " (NOI). T h e debt service is not part o f this e q u a tion; however, this debt service m u s t b e paid from t h e
c a s h f l o w (profit).
Once you have a value for the NOI the value of a c o m mercial property is calculated using the " C A P rate". In t h e
real estate industry t h e e x p e r t s will look a t other similar
p r o p e r t i e s w h i c h h a v e s o l d a n d t h e y will t a k e t h e N O I o f
these properties and divide this v a l u e by the purchase
price. T h e result is w h a t t h e industry refers t o a s a C A P
r a t e . T h e h i s t o r i c a l s a l e s o f s i m i l a r p r o p e r t i e s will c r e a t e
a range o f C A P rate v a l u e s which investors can then a p ply t o d e t e r m i n e t h e v a l u e o f a p o t e n t i a l p r o p e r t y .
T h e f o r m u l a t o d e t e r m i n e v a l u e o r C A P r a t e is: V a l u e =
N O I / C A P . T h i s is a very straight forward a n d relatively
simple process which one would think would provide a
fairly accurate v a l u e . T h e challenge is that t h e N O I c a n
be easily m a n i p u l a t e d . W h e n a seller is selling a property

Capital v s Maintenance
M a n y people don't understand the difference bet w e e n a capital e x p e n s e a n d a m a i n t e n a n c e e x pense so I thought Iwould share with y o u s o m e
insight into t h e difference b e t w e e n t h e t w o , a s
well a s the pros and cons of each. I d o need t o
s t r e s s t h a t I a m not a n a c c o u n t a n t a n d I a m n o t
providing accounting advice s o y o u will n e e d t o
verify this information I a m about to s h a r e with
y o u r o w n a c c o u n t a n t t o s e e h o w it b e s t s e r v e s
you.
W h e n y o u replace a set of taps in a n apartment a t
a cost of $ 2 0 0 then this e x p e n s e w o u l d b e classified a s a m a i n t e n a n c e e x p e n s e . O t h e r e x a m p l e s
of maintenance expenses could be the changing
of light fixtures, replacing a c o u n t e r top, replacing
a n e w tub surround, replacing carpet in a bedroom/living room, repairing a broken window, appliance repairs, etc. N o w at t h e year e n d your acc o u n t a n t will a d d u p all o f t h e s e m a i n t e n a n c e e x penses a n d u s e 1 0 0 % of this n u m b e r to reduce
the taxable income of the property for that year.
W h e n y o u o w n a rental property y o u should budget for these types of m a i n t e n a n c e e x p e n s e s o n a n
annual basis. I w a s taught to budget a n y w h e r e
from $ 5 0 0 to $ 7 0 0 per unit per year for these
types of maintenance expense, depending upon
the a g e a n d condition of t h e property. T h e last
thing y o u w a n t to do isbuy a property a n d not
take these types of e x p e n s e s into consideration in
y o u r b u d g e t b e c a u s e this will directly i m p a c t y o u r
profitability.
A c a p i t a l e x p e n s e i s u s u a l l y (if n o t a l w a y s ) m u c h
more expensive than a maintenance expense.
S o m e e a s y e x a m p l e s of a capital e x p e n s e w o u l d

a m u c h l o n g e r life e x p e c t a n c y t h a n a m a i n t e nance item; therefore, since the benefit is m u c h

to e x p e n s e t h i s c o s t o v e r t h e e x p e c t e d life o f t h e
improvement. F o r example a resurfaced parking
lot c a n b e e x p e n s e d a t 8 % p e r y e a r , a n e w f e n c e
at 1 0 % per year other items m a y be 2 0 % per year

conceive and believe, it can achieve."
Here's a classic example. In 1961 w h e n J o h n F.
K e n n e d y a n n o u n c e d , "Before this decade is out,
America will land a man on the Moon and return him
safely to the Earth." At that time the Americans were
getting badly beaten in the space race by the Russians who had launched the first satellite in 1957
and then accomplished the first manned orbit of the
earth. For America to land a man on the moon was
both improbable and for most people, totally inconceivable. And yet the vision was brought into being.
C h o o s i n g a big v i s i o n a n d living i n t o it w o r k s b e cause:
1 . ) It i n s p i r e s a n d e n e r g i z e s y o u , c a l l i n g y o u f o r t h t o
m a k e things happen and deliver your best possible
performance;
2. ) E v e n w h e n y o u d o n ' t k n o w w h a t n e e d s t o b e
d o n e o r h o w t o d o it, y o u r c o m m i t m e n t c r e a t e s a t e n sion that attracts the solutions and resources y o u
need;
3. ) Y o u r vision attracts a n d inspires o t h e r s t o j o i n
your cause. W h e n you are committed to something
bigger t h a n yourself, other people will s h o w up a n d
join with you.
O f c o u r s e t h e r e will b e complications, m i s t a k e s a n d
disasters along the w a y to your vision. Eight Americ a n a s t r o n a u t s d i e d in t r a i n i n g b e f o r e t h e s u c c e s s f u l
l u n a r l a n d i n g . B u t if y o u t r y t o a v o i d m i s t a k e s y o u w i l l
end up playing small and never achieve anything
worthwhile. Y o u m u s t look at problems a s t e m p o rary setbacks and not a s failures. A s the C h i n e s e
p r o v e r b g o e s , "A blow that does not kill me makes
me stronger."
O n e of m y favorite quotes about vision c o m e s from
the G o r d o n Lightfoot song, Canadian Railroad Trilog y . T h e l y r i c s g o , "They looked in the future and
what did they see? They saw an iron road running
from the sea to the sea." I n 1 8 7 1 t h e t r a n s c o n t i n e n tal C P R w a s a bold, a u d a c i o u s d r e a m that w a s t e c h n i c a l l y u n a c h i e v a b l e . F o u r t e e n y e a r s l a t e r it w a s a
reality.
S o l o o k in y o u r f u t u r e a n d w h a t d o y o u s e e ? F o r g e t
about projecting a h e a d based o n the limitations of
today. Start from a blank slate and imagine a future
that inspires y o u . A s long a s y o u c a n fully "conceive
a n d b e l i e v e " it, y o u c a n h a v e it. A n d e v e n i f it d o e s n ' t
w o r k o u t a s y o u h o p e o r p l a n , if y o u t a k e b o l d a c t i o n

beyond what you can imagine.

BY JULY ONO

W h a t is t h ebest w a yt o hold i t ?
T h e r e is n o right o r w r o n g , g o o d o r b a d a n s w e r
o n h o w t o h o l d y o u r r e a l e s t a t e i n v e s t m e n t . It
c o m e s d o w n t o what your intention a n d purpose
is. T h e r e a r e o n l y t w o e n t i t i e s t h a t a r e p e r m i t t e d
to o w n r e a l e s t a t e : a p e r s o n o r a c o m p a n y . If a
p e r s o n o r p e o p l e a r e n o t g o i n g o n title, t h e n a
corporate entity is t h e only other choice. E v e n a
T r u s t c a n n o t directly o w n real estate.
This is w h y a Joint V e n t u r e Partnership is o n e of
the simplest and cost effective w a y s t o hold a
real estate investment that involves multiple
owner-investors. A joint venture agreement outlines t h e r o l e s a n d r e s p o n s i b i l i t i e s o f all t h e parties including t h epro-rated ownership o f the inv e s t m e n t and w h a t t o d o in the event o f a dispute, death o r incapacitation of investors o r the
m a n a g i n g partner. W h e n all p a r t i e s a r e o n title,
this m e a n s that e v e r y o n e takes o n 1 0 0 % o f the
liability. T o b e clear, this is n o t a p r o - r a t e d port i o n o f y o u r p e r c e n t a g e o f t h e liability. E a c h indiv i d u a l i n v e s t o r is t a k i n g o n a l l o f t h e liability. T h e
b a n k will e n s u r e this by having e v e r y o n e sign a
personal guarantee. This is the bank's w a y of
mitigating their risk. A n y a n d all c a s h f l o w that is
g e n e r a t e d b y t h e i n v e s t m e n t is d e e m e d i n c o m e
a n d is t a x e d at y o u r personal marginal t a x rate.
In o r d e r t o p r o v i d e liability p r o t e c t i o n t o i n v e s tors, the G e n e r a l Partner - Limited Partner (GLLP) structures w e r e quite fashionable in the
1990's and early 2000's. T h e real estate investm e n t would b e held b y a n entity referred t o a s a
bare estate trustee. This m e a n s that the corporation is holding land a n dis not deriving any inc o m e f r o m it. A b a r e e s t a t e m e a n s t h a t t h e
s t r u c t u r e i s a f l o w t h r o u g h . T h e r e f o r e , it d o e s n o t
pay t a x e s b e c a u s e there is n o i n c o m e t o b e
taxed. T h e investors b u yunits in the Limited
Partnership and are referred t o a s unit-holders.
The General Partner of a Limited Partnership
a s s u m e s 1 0 0 % o f t h e liability. T h e liability o f t h e
Limited P a r t n e r s is limited t o their pro-rated port i o n o f t h e i n v e s t m e n t o n l y if t h e y r e m a i n s i l e n t
p a r t n e r s . T h e G e n e r a l P a r t n e r t a k e s o n all t h e
risk b e c a u s e t h e G P h a n d l e s the day-to-day ope r a t i o n s o f t h e b u s i n e s s . If a L i m i t e d P a r t n e r
w e r e t o b e c o m e involved in daily operations a n d
making business decisions, they lose their limited liability s t a t u s .

The cash flow generated from a G P - L P
structure m u s t b e distributed t o the unit
holders. T h e L P is n o t
p e r m i t t e d t o r e t a i n t h e i n c o m e o r it w i l l b e t a x e d a t
the highest marginal tax rate. That's because the
i n c o m e derived f r o m a n L P is d e e m e d passive i n c o m e , n o t a c t i v e i n c o m e . T h e g o v e r n m e n t h a s different rules f o rpassive income versus active inc o m e . A s y o u c a n s e e f r o m t h e link b e l o w o n t h e
2015 Corporate Income T a x Rates, t h eFederal tax
rate o f 1 1 % f o ra C C P C active small business is
1 1 % versus the investment income rate o f 3 4 . 7 % .
http://www.taxtips.ca/smallbusiness/
corporatetax7corporate-tax-rates-2015.htm
In o r d e r t o b e c o n s i d e r e d a n active b u s i n e s s , a real
estate holding would need t o employ five full-time
e m p l o y e e s and o n e part-time employee t o enjoy
the corporate tax rate. T h i s active business status
w o u l d n e e d t o b e corroborated b y T 4 slips. A n a c tive b u s i n e s s is referred t o a s C C P C f o rshort o r
Canadian Controlled Private Corporation. Your
c o m p a n y w i l l n o t q u a l i f y i f it i s c o n t r o l l e d d i r e c t l y o r
indirectly b y non-residents o r b y a public corporation. T h e r e a s o n for this is b e c a u s e a C C P C is
t a x e d a t t h e l o w e s t f e d e r a l t a x r a t e o f 1 1 %. G o i n g
f o r w a r d , t h e s e r a t e s will i n c r e m e n t a l l y d e c r e a s e t o
9 % by 2019. Corporations receive favorable taxation because small businesses stimulate the econo m y . L o w e r taxation e n c o u r a g e s people t o start
businesses.

R E A L T Y L E G A L I T I E S WITH B A R R Y M C G U I R E

W H Y T H E ULTRA-RICH LIKE REAL
ESTATE
Tougher economic times make usquestion why
w e invest in real estate. Y o u r property v a l u e s
m a y b e going d o w n , tenants a r eharder t o find
a n d n o b o d y k n o w s if t h e p r i c e o f oil i s c o m i n g
back anytime soon. Perhaps s o m e of your friends
are being foreclosed on. For a lot of people in
provinces that d e p e n d o n oilf o r commodities,
these a r enotnecessarily pleasant times.
W h e n t h e r e isc h a o s all a r o u n d , s o m e t i m e s it's
beneficial t o t a k e a step b a c k a n d revisit t h e reas o n s w h y w e think real estate isa great investment.
B e t t e r yet, let's t a k e a p e e k a t w h y t h e ultra-rich
(let's d e f i n e ultra-rich a s p e o p l e h a v e $ 1 0 million
plus t o invest) like real estate. Ultra-rich folks a r e
ultra-rich f o ra reason. T h e y m a k e investments
that are g o o d over the long haul and that don't
lose t h e m m o n e y . Let's discuss this further. A s a
starting point, here's w h a t S i m o n Jochlin o f S t e n n e r Z o h n y I n v e s t m e n t P a r t n e r s (part o f R i c h a r d s o n G M P in V a n c o u v e r ) had t o say.
"Real estate is generally accepted as an alternative investment. It has the characteristics of an
inflation hedge: yield, leverage and capital gains.
It does well in upwardly trending markets, it pays
you to wait during market corrections and typically it lags equities in market declines-it buys you
time to assess the market."
T h i n k about that... yield, leverage and capital
gains. In other w o r d s y o u r m o n e y is w o r k i n g f o r
y o u a n d paying w h a t iseffectively a n interest rate
o n capital invested, y o u might h a v e t o only invest
2 0 % of the value of a piece o f property and,
w h e n y o u sell, y o u g e t preferred tax t r e a t m e n t b y
paying only capital gains. W h a t ' s not t o like?
W h a t about the other three factors? Yes, in upward trending markets property values g o up; in
d o w n w a r d trending markets, properly purchased
and m a n a g e d real estate does pay t o wait. Property values m a y n o tb e moving u p a n y m o r e but
y o u still h a v e l o w v a c a n c y a n d p o s i t i v e c a s h f l o w .
Y o u c a nafford t o wait. A n d since ultra-rich investors also invest in t h e stock market, they note that
w h e r e the stock market can bounce u p and d o w n

frantically, property v a l u e s
are m u c h m o r e stable.
T r e n d s a r eshallower giving
y o u time t o really think about your investment.
Darren C o l e m a n isa senior vice president a n d
portfolio m a n a g e r at R a y m o n d J a m e s L T D in T o ronto. Here's what h e h a d t o s a y about real estate
and high net worth individuals.
"real estate is very popular and one of the reasons,
in my opinion, is that investors can actually see and
touch their investment". M r . C o l e m a n a l s o t h i n k s
that real e s t a t e investors, w h e t h e r t h e y fit in t h e
u l t r a - w e a l t h y o r p e r h a p s h a v e a little l e s s m o n e y ,
a r e actually m o r e logical t h a n stock m a r k e t invest o r s . M r . C o l e m a n s a y s , "for example, if you own a
rental condo, and the one across the hall goes on
sale for 30% less than what you think it's worth,
you wouldn't automatically put yours on the market
and sell too because you think there's a problem.
Indeed, you may actually buy the other condo. And
yet when a stock drops on the market, instead of
thinking of buying more, most people automatically
become fearful and think they should sell."
Going back t o the benefit o f leverage Mr. C o l e m a n
a d d s : "banks love to lend against it (real estate).
Over time, this lets you own a property with a much
smaller investment than if you had to buy all of it at
once."Again,
a s a n investment professional w h o
sees many types o f investments, Mr.Coleman believes buying real estate with a 5 t o 1 leverage
based o n 2 0 % down isa much safer investment

Keeping Costs Down - CASHFLOW
After years of investing in
real estate with s o m e deg r e e of success, I find
t h a t a lot o f m y c o n v e r s a tions turn to real estate. I
t h i n k it d r i v e s m y w i f e a
little c r a z y ; s h e a l w a y s
m e n t i o n s a t s o c i a l g a t h e r i n g s t h a t if y o u t a l k t o m y

Q: I only h a v e a certain a m o u n t of capital to invest. H o w d o I build a portfolio of properties without
running out of cash to do so?
A : T h e s i m p l e a n s w e r is h a r d w o r k & p r o p e r d u e diligence!

w a s buying undervalued property. T h e n , I w a s prepared to do as m u c h of the work as I could myself to
force appreciation while keeping renovation costs
d o w n to a m i n i m u m . Between these two strategies,
I w a s able to either re-finance the property a n d get
t h e m a j o r i t y o f m y c a s h o u t o r s e l l it t o g r o w m y
bankroll for the next opportunity.

t o m u l t i - p u r p o s e w i t h a b a s e m e n t s u i t e ; if d o n e correctly, y o u c a n s e e s o m e s u b s t a n t i a l g a i n s in p r o p e r t y v a l u e . M a y b e t h e b e s t p a r t i s if y o u m e s s u p y o u r
